Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-20805")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-20805")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

[LLM] n8n CVE-2026-21858 (Ni8mare) Vulnerable Version Inventory Reconnaissance High
Identifies hosts running self-hosted n8n versions prior to 1.121.0/1.121.3 exposed to unauthenticated RCE via Form Webhook Content-Type confusion. Catches the attack surface BEFORE exploitation and feeds patching priorities. Rationale: Article explicitly names CVE-2026-21858 and affected version ranges (<1.121.0, <1.121.3). Vuln-management telemetry gives an unambiguous, almost-zero-FP signal for asset exposure before any exploit traffic arrives. Internet-facing pivot prioritises the most urgent hosts.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities.Vulnerabilities
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where CveId =~ "CVE-2026-21858" or (SoftwareName has "n8n" and VulnerabilitySeverityLevel in~ ("Critical","High"))
| join kind=leftouter (DeviceInfo | summarize arg_max(Timestamp, OSPlatform, IsInternetFacing, PublicIP) by DeviceId) on DeviceId
| project Timestamp, DeviceId, DeviceName, OSPlatform, SoftwareVendor, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate, IsInternetFacing, PublicIP
| order by IsInternetFacing desc, Timestamp desc
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where CveId =~ "CVE-2026-21858" or (SoftwareName has "n8n" and VulnerabilitySeverityLevel in~ ("Critical","High"))
| join kind=leftouter (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d) | summarize arg_max(TimeGenerated, OSPlatform, IsInternetFacing, PublicIP) by DeviceId) on DeviceId
| project TimeGenerated, DeviceId, DeviceName, OSPlatform, SoftwareVendor, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate, IsInternetFacing, PublicIP
| order by IsInternetFacing desc, TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] n8n Node Process Spawning Shell Interpreter (CVE-2026-21858 Post-Exploit RCE) Exploitation High
Detects the n8n node.js workflow engine spawning /bin/sh, bash, cmd.exe, or PowerShell — the unambiguous endpoint signature of CVE-2026-21858 unauthenticated RCE or abuse of the Execute Command workflow node. Self-hosted n8n almost never legitimately spawns OS shells unless Execute Command is explicitly enabled. Rationale: Article states attackers can 'Execute arbitrary commands' via the Form Webhook flaw and explicitly calls out the Execute Command node as a high-risk feature to disable. Any node.js process whose parent command line contains 'n8n' spawning a shell interpreter is either active exploitation or unauthorized use of Execute Command — both warrant immediate triage. Parent-child pairing keeps FPs minimal even in environments where node.js runs other workloads.
ATT&CKT1190 T1059.004 T1059.003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node","node.exe","npm","npx")
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine has "n8n"
   or InitiatingProcessFolderPath has_any ("/n8n","\\n8n","/.n8n","\\.n8n")
| where FileName in~ ("sh","bash","dash","ash","zsh","ksh","cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          ParentCmd   = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildImage  = FolderPath,
          ChildCmd    = ProcessCommandLine,
          SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ParentProcessName in~ ("node","node.exe","npm","npx")
| where ParentProcessCommandLine has "n8n" or ActingProcessCommandLine has "n8n"
| where ActingProcessName in~ ("sh","bash","dash","ash","zsh","ksh","cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: n8n Workflow Engine Spawning Shell Interpreter (CVE-2026-21858)
id: 6f3a2d51-9b4e-4a17-8b9c-2e5f8d7a1c93
description: Detects n8n node.js workflow engine spawning a shell or command interpreter, indicating exploitation of CVE-2026-21858 unauthenticated RCE via Form Webhook Content-Type confusion or abuse of the Execute Command workflow node.
references:
  - https://www.aikido.dev/blog/n8n-rce-vulnerability-cve-2026-21858
author: Detection Engineering
date: 2026/01/08
tags:
  - attack.t1190
  - attack.initial_access
  - attack.t1059.003
  - attack.t1059.004

logsource:
  category: process_creation
detection:
  selection_parent:
    ParentImage|endswith:
      - '/node'
      - '\node.exe'
      - '/npm'
      - '/npx'
    ParentCommandLine|contains: 'n8n'
  selection_child:
    Image|endswith:
      - '/sh'
      - '/bash'
      - '/dash'
      - '/ash'
      - '/zsh'
      - '/ksh'
      - '\cmd.exe'
      - '\powershell.exe'
      - '\pwsh.exe'
  condition: selection_parent and selection_child
level: high
source:(linux.auditd OR windows.sysmon) (@process.parent.command_line:*n8n* OR @ParentCommandLine:*n8n*) (@process.executable.path:(/bin/sh OR /usr/bin/sh OR /bin/bash OR /usr/bin/bash OR /bin/dash OR /usr/bin/dash OR /bin/zsh) OR @Image:(*\\cmd.exe OR *\\CMD.EXE OR *\\powershell.exe OR *\\PowerShell.exe OR *\\POWERSHELL.EXE OR *\\pwsh.exe OR *\\PWSH.EXE))
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 ParentBaseFileName=/^(node|npm|npx)(\.exe)?$/i ParentCommandLine=/n8n/i ImageFileName=/(\/|\\)(sh|bash|dash|ash|zsh|ksh|cmd\.exe|powershell\.exe|pwsh\.exe)$/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ParentBaseFileName, ImageFileName, CommandLine], function=count())

[LLM] n8n Form/Webhook Endpoint Hit with JSON Content-Type Confusion Payload (CVE-2026-21858 Exploit Attempt) Delivery Medium
Detects HTTP POST/PUT requests to n8n /form/* or /webhook/* endpoints that carry an application/json Content-Type instead of the expected multipart/form-data — the precise exploit signature of CVE-2026-21858. Also flags requests whose body/query contain file-traversal markers (../, /etc/passwd, database.sqlite) targeting the arbitrary-file-read primitive Cyera demonstrated. Rationale: Article identifies the root cause precisely: Content-Type confusion where attackers send application/json to a Form Webhook endpoint that expects multipart/form-data, plus chained file-traversal to read /etc/passwd and the SQLite database. The path strings (/form, /webhook), the wrong content-type, and the file targets are all article-verbatim IOCs. Defender XDR lacks HTTP reverse-proxy telemetry by default, so defender_kql is intentionally empty; Falcon FDR likewise doesn't ship HTTP access logs.
ATT&CKT1190 T1083 T1212
Data sourcesWeb.Web
let suspiciousPaths = dynamic(["/form/","/webhook/","/form-trigger/","/form-test/"]);
let traversalMarkers = dynamic(["../","%2e%2e%2f","%2e%2e/","/etc/passwd","/etc/shadow","database.sqlite","config.json",".n8n/config"]);
(union isfuzzy=true
  (CommonSecurityLog
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
   | where RequestMethod in~ ("POST","PUT")
   | where RequestURL has_any (suspiciousPaths)
   | where RequestURL has_any (traversalMarkers) or RequestContext has "application/json"
   | project TimeGenerated, Source=DeviceVendor, Target=DestinationHostName, ClientIP=SourceIP, RequestMethod, RequestURL, UserAgent=RequestClientApplication, Status=tostring(EventOutcome)),
  (W3CIISLog
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
   | where csMethod in~ ("POST","PUT")
   | where csUriStem has_any (suspiciousPaths)
   | where csUriQuery has_any (traversalMarkers) or csUriStem has_any (traversalMarkers)
   | project TimeGenerated, Source="IIS", Target=sSiteName, ClientIP=cIP, RequestMethod=csMethod, RequestURL=strcat(csUriStem, "?", csUriQuery), UserAgent=csUserAgent, Status=tostring(scStatus)),
  (Syslog
   | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
   | where ProcessName has_any ("nginx","haproxy","caddy","traefik","n8n") or SyslogMessage has "n8n"
   | where SyslogMessage matches regex @"(?i)(POST|PUT)\s+/(form|webhook|form-trigger|form-test)/"
   | where SyslogMessage has_any (traversalMarkers) or SyslogMessage has "application/json"
   | project TimeGenerated, Source="Syslog", Target=Computer, ClientIP=HostIP, RequestMethod="", RequestURL=SyslogMessage, UserAgent="", Status=tostring(SeverityLevel))
)
| order by TimeGenerated desc
(source:nginx OR source:apache OR source:iis OR source:haproxy OR source:caddy OR source:traefik) (@http.url_details.path:*/form/* OR @http.url_details.path:*/webhook/* OR @http.url_details.path:*/form-trigger/* OR @http.url_details.path:*/form-test/*) @http.method:(POST OR PUT) (@http.request.headers.content-type:*application/json* OR @http.url:*..%2F* OR @http.url:*../* OR @http.url:*%2Fetc%2Fpasswd* OR @http.url:*/etc/passwd* OR @http.url:*database.sqlite*)

| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`

| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)

Stealers / wallet drainers read keystore.json, MetaMask, Exodus, Atomic, Phantom data.
ATT&CKT1005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where FolderPath has_any (@"\Ethereum\keystore\", @"\Bitcoin\", @"\Exodus\", @"\Electrum\wallets\", @"\MetaMask\", @"\Phantom\", @"\Atomic\Local Storage\")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath, FileName, ActionType

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where TargetFilePath has_any (@"\Ethereum\keystore\", @"\Bitcoin\",
                                  @"\Exodus\", @"\Electrum\wallets\",
                                  @"\MetaMask\", @"\Phantom\",
                                  @"\Atomic\Local Storage\")
| where ActingProcessName !in~ ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe",
                                  "electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
          TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, EventType

title: Crypto-Wallet Keystore Access by Non-Wallet Process
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d02
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers / wallet drainers read keystore files from MetaMask,
    Exodus, Atomic, Phantom, Bitcoin, Electrum, Ethereum-cli wallets.
    Filtering out the legitimate wallet binaries leaves only attacker
    activity.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1005/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1005
    - attack.collection
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Ethereum\keystore\'
            - '\Bitcoin\'
            - '\Exodus\'
            - '\Electrum\wallets\'
            - '\MetaMask\'
            - '\Phantom\'
            - '\Atomic\Local Storage\'
    filter_legit:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\MetaMask.exe'
            - '\Exodus.exe'
            - '\Atomic.exe'
            - '\electrum.exe'
            - '\Bitcoin.exe'
            - '\Phantom.exe'
    condition: selection_path and not filter_legit
falsepositives:
    - Backup software
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Ethereum\keystore\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Bitcoin\wallet.dat"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Exodus\exodus.wallet*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Electrum\wallets\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\MetaMask\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Phantom\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Atomic\Local Storage\*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs Actions on Objectives High
Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies SQLite from Chrome/Edge/Firefox.
ATT&CKT1539 T1555.003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents

| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where FolderPath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\", @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\", @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")
| where FileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath, FileName, ActionType

// ASIM ImFileEvent — covers MDE, Sysmon, AuditD, Defender for Cloud, etc.
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where TargetFilePath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\",
                                  @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\",
                                  @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")
| where TargetFileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")
| where ActingProcessName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
          TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, EventType

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-21858")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-21858")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium

    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

Beyond Detection: Building a Resilient Software Supply Chain (Lessons from the Shai-Hulud Post-Mortem)

Snyk
2026-01-08 1 use case 2 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

The Shai-Hulud npm incident exposed the limitations of reactive security in modern software supply chains. To survive the next major attack, organizations must shift toward a multi-layered strategy of proactive prevention, real-time intelligence, and automated action.

ATT&CKT1190 T1195.002
Click any ATT&CK pill below to open it on the Matrix
Phase 1
Reconnaissance
Detected
Phase 2
Weaponization
—
Phase 3
Delivery
Detected
Phase 4
Exploitation
Detected
Phase 5
Installation
Detected
Phase 6
Command & Control
Detected
Phase 7
Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.

[LLM] NeoShadow npm loader: node.exe spawning MSBuild with a .proj from %TEMP% Installation High
Hunts the Stage-0/1 execution pivot of the NeoShadow campaign — an npm postinstall script (node.exe) that drops a randomly-named .proj file in %TEMP% and invokes the .NET v4.0.30319 MSBuild.exe with the article's exact /nologo /noconsolelogger flags. The node.exe→MSBuild.exe parent/child relationship combined with a Temp .proj path is essentially never benign on developer workstations. Rationale: Combines five article-specific strings: parent=node.exe (npm postinstall), child=MSBuild.exe at the literal .NET v4.0.30319 path, .proj extension, %TEMP% folder, and the unusual paired /nologo /noconsolelogger silent-execution flags hardcoded in the malware. The combination has no legitimate developer use case. Pre-extracted IOCs from the Aikido analysis used directly.
ATT&CKT1127.001 T1195.002 T1059.005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName =~ "node.exe"
| where FileName =~ "MSBuild.exe"
| where FolderPath has @"\Microsoft.NET\Framework64\v4.0.30319\"
| where ProcessCommandLine has ".proj"
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any (@"\Temp\", @"\AppData\Local\Temp\")
| where ProcessCommandLine has "/nologo" and ProcessCommandLine has "/noconsolelogger"
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine, SHA256, InitiatingProcessParentFileName
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith @"\node.exe"
| where NewProcessName endswith @"\MSBuild.exe"
| where NewProcessName has @"\Microsoft.NET\Framework64\v4.0.30319\"
| where CommandLine has ".proj"
| where CommandLine has_any (@"\Temp\", @"\AppData\Local\Temp\")
| where CommandLine has "/nologo" and CommandLine has "/noconsolelogger"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName, NewProcessName, CommandLine, TokenElevationType

description: Detects node.exe (npm postinstall) launching MSBuild.exe from the .NET v4.0.30319 framework path with a .proj file in %TEMP% and the article's signature /nologo /noconsolelogger flags - matching the NeoShadow loader behaviour.

[LLM] NeoShadow persistence: config.proj written to %APPDATA%\Microsoft\CLR Installation High
Detects the secondary-artifact persistence path used by NeoShadow's Stage-1 payload, which downloads /_next/data/config.json from the C2 and writes it to %APPDATA%\Microsoft\CLR\config.proj. This precise folder/filename combination is created by the malware via Directory.CreateDirectory + File.WriteAllText and is not used by any standard Microsoft component. Rationale: The exact path %APPDATA%\Microsoft\CLR\config.proj is hardcoded in the C# code embedded in the MSBuild payload (Path.Combine(...ApplicationData, "Microsoft", "CLR", "config.proj")) and is not a standard Windows or .NET CLR location - making it a near-zero-FP indicator of NeoShadow infection.
ATT&CKT1547 T1127.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated", "FileModified", "FileRenamed")
| where FolderPath has @"\AppData\Roaming\Microsoft\CLR\"
| where FileName =~ "config.proj"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, FolderPath, FileName, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessParentFileName, FileOriginUrl, FileOriginIP
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventType in ("FileCreated", "FileModified")
| where TargetFilePath has @"\AppData\Roaming\Microsoft\CLR\"
| where TargetFileName =~ "config.proj"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, TargetFileSHA256, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine

[LLM] NeoShadow C2 / Etherscan blockchain C2-resolution traffic Command & Control High
Hunts NeoShadow's distinctive command-and-control retrieval channels: (a) the Etherscan eth_call to attacker contract 0x13660FD7Edc862377e799b0Caf68f99a2939B5cC with function selector 0xd6bd8727 used to fetch the dynamic C2 URL from on-chain data, and (b) the hardcoded fallback C2 metrics-flow.com / 80.78.22.206 including the masquerading /assets/js/analytics.min.js payload URL. Rationale: Three orthogonal article-specific anchors make this near-zero-FP: (1) the literal C2 IP 80.78.22.206 and domain metrics-flow.com, (2) the unique Ethereum contract address 0x13660FD7Edc862377e799b0Caf68f99a2939B5cC and its non-standard function selector 0xd6bd8727 used as a dead-drop resolver against Etherscan, (3) the masquerading payload paths /assets/js/analytics.min.js and /_next/data/config.json on that domain. Sigma omitted: detection spans multiple log sources (network/dns/proxy) and can't be expressed in a single-event Sigma rule.
ATT&CKT1102.001 T1071.001 T1568
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Web.Web
union
  (DeviceNetworkEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where RemoteIP == "80.78.22.206"
        or RemoteUrl has "metrics-flow.com"
        or RemoteUrl has "0x13660FD7Edc862377e799b0Caf68f99a2939B5cC"
        or RemoteUrl has "0xd6bd8727"
        or RemoteUrl has "/_next/data/config.json"
        or RemoteUrl has "/assets/js/analytics.min.js"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteIP, RemoteUrl, RemotePort, Source="Network"),
  (DeviceEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where ActionType == "DnsQueryResponse"
    | extend QueryName = tostring(parse_json(AdditionalFields).QueryName)
    | where QueryName endswith "metrics-flow.com"
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, RemoteUrl=QueryName, Source="DNS")
| order by Timestamp desc
union isfuzzy=true
  (CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where DestinationIP == "80.78.22.206"
        or DestinationHostName has "metrics-flow.com"
        or RequestURL has_any ("0x13660FD7Edc862377e799b0Caf68f99a2939B5cC", "0xd6bd8727", "/assets/js/analytics.min.js", "/_next/data/config.json")
    | project TimeGenerated, SourceIP, SourceHostName, DestinationIP, DestinationHostName, RequestURL, DeviceVendor, DeviceProduct, Src="FW/Proxy"),
  (DnsEvents
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where Name endswith "metrics-flow.com"
    | project TimeGenerated, Computer, ClientIP, Name, QueryType, Src="DNS"),

    | where Url has_any ("metrics-flow.com", "0x13660FD7Edc862377e799b0Caf68f99a2939B5cC", "0xd6bd8727")
    | project TimeGenerated, SrcHostname, SrcIpAddr, DstIpAddr, Url, HttpStatusCode, Src="ASIM")
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

ATT&CKT1059.001 T1027

DeviceProcessEvents

| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")

| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\pwsh.exe"
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName,
          CommandLine, ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

status: experimental
description: |
    PowerShell launched with -EncodedCommand, base64 strings, hidden
    window, no-profile, bypass policy, IEX / Invoke-Expression / Net

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files

                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")

          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName

| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

The Holiday Whisper: Shai-Hulud 3.0

Snyk
2025-12-29 5 use cases 3 techniques 5 kill-chain phases detected

A refined variant of the Shai-Hulud malware, dubbed The Golden Path, has been discovered targeting the npm ecosystem during the holiday season. Security teams are encouraged to prioritize structural hardening, such as disabling lifecycle scripts, to mitigate risks during this testing phase.

ATT&CKT1027 T1190 T1195.002

Phase 7
Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → [LLM] Compromised npm package @vietmoney/react-big-calendar@0.26.2 installation (Shai-Hulud 3.0)
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] npm/yarn/pnpm/bun lifecycle hook spawning shell or network LOLBin
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] Shai-Hulud 3.0 'Goldox-T3chs' GitHub exfiltration marker observed
[LLM] Compromised npm package @vietmoney/react-big-calendar@0.26.2 installation (Shai-Hulud 3.0) Delivery High
Detects the install/use of the specific malicious npm package version named in the Snyk advisory. Catches package introduction across npm/yarn/pnpm/bun on Windows + Linux developer and CI hosts, including the Bun runtime the v3.0 variant explicitly targets. Rationale: Article-specific package identifier `@vietmoney/react-big-calendar` (and the targeted v0.26.2) is a unique high-fidelity string; the broader Snyk family of Shai-Hulud variants has been independently corroborated by multiple security vendors (referenced in claude-mem observation 1325, see also OX Security and Aikido write-ups of prior Shai-Hulud iterations). Bun runtime is explicitly added to the binary list because the article calls out the v3.0 "patch" specifically fixing Bun compatibility. Cross-checked against: • https://snyk.io/blog/shai-hulud-3-0/
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.007
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
let pkg = "@vietmoney/react-big-calendar";
let procHits = DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where (FileName in~ ("node.exe","npm.cmd","npm.exe","pnpm.cmd","pnpm.exe","yarn.cmd","yarn.exe","bun.exe"))
         or (InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","npm.cmd","npm.exe","pnpm.cmd","pnpm.exe","yarn.cmd","yarn.exe","bun.exe"))
    | where ProcessCommandLine has pkg or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has pkg
    | extend Evidence = "process", Detail = coalesce(ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessCommandLine)
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, Evidence, Detail, FileName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath;
let fileHits = DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FolderPath has @"node_modules\@vietmoney\react-big-calendar" or FolderPath has "node_modules/@vietmoney/react-big-calendar"
    | extend Evidence = "file", Detail = strcat(FolderPath, "\\", FileName)
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, Evidence, Detail, FileName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath;
procHits
| union fileHits
| order by Timestamp desc
let pkg = "@vietmoney/react-big-calendar";
let procHits = ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where ActingProcessName has_any ("node.exe","npm.cmd","npm.exe","pnpm.cmd","pnpm.exe","yarn.cmd","yarn.exe","bun.exe")
         or ParentProcessName has_any ("node.exe","npm.cmd","pnpm.cmd","yarn.cmd","bun.exe")
    | where ActingProcessCommandLine has pkg or ParentProcessCommandLine has pkg
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, Evidence="process", Detail=ActingProcessCommandLine, ActingProcessName, ParentProcessName;
let legacyHits = SecurityEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d) and EventID == 4688
    | where CommandLine has pkg
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname=Computer, ActorUsername=SubjectUserName, Evidence="process", Detail=CommandLine, ActingProcessName=NewProcessName, ParentProcessName;
let fileHits = ImFileEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where TargetFilePath has @"node_modules\@vietmoney\react-big-calendar" or TargetFilePath has "node_modules/@vietmoney/react-big-calendar"
    | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, Evidence="file", Detail=TargetFilePath, ActingProcessName, ParentProcessName="";
procHits | union legacyHits, fileHits | order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] npm/yarn/pnpm/bun lifecycle hook spawning shell or network LOLBin Installation Medium
Hunts the primary execution vector named in the article: postinstall and preinstall lifecycle hooks invoking a shell, downloader, or git client. Article-specific because Snyk explicitly recommends `ignore-scripts=true` as the structural mitigation — the detection mirrors the exact behaviour that flag would have blocked. Rationale: Article anchors: the `postinstall` and `preinstall` lifecycle hooks plus the `ignore-scripts=true` recommendation directly map to a parent-child detection where the parent is the package manager invoking a lifecycle script and the child is anything capable of network egress or local persistence. Bun included because the article calls out the Bun runtime patch. Cross-vendor coverage of prior Shai-Hulud variants (claude-mem 1283, 1284) confirms the same hook-based execution pattern. Cross-checked against: • https://snyk.io/blog/shai-hulud-3-0/
ATT&CKT1059.007 T1546 T1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","npm.cmd","pnpm.cmd","yarn.cmd","bun.exe","npm","pnpm","yarn","bun","node")
| where InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any ("postinstall","preinstall","npm-cli.js","pnpm-cli","yarn install","npm install","npm ci","pnpm install","bun install","run-script")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","git.exe","schtasks.exe","reg.exe","bash","sh","python.exe","python","python3")
   or (FileName =~ "node.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has_any (" -e ","--eval","child_process","trufflehog","http.request","https.request"))
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| extend ParentHook = case(
    InitiatingProcessCommandLine has "postinstall", "postinstall",
    InitiatingProcessCommandLine has "preinstall", "preinstall",
    "install-context")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, ParentHook,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ChildImage = FileName, ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine,
          FolderPath, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
let LifecycleParents = dynamic(["node.exe","npm.cmd","pnpm.cmd","yarn.cmd","bun.exe","npm","pnpm","yarn","bun","node"]);
let SuspectChildren = dynamic(["powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","git.exe","schtasks.exe","reg.exe","bash","sh","python.exe","python","python3"]);
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ParentProcessName has_any (LifecycleParents)
| where ParentProcessCommandLine has_any ("postinstall","preinstall","npm-cli.js","pnpm-cli","yarn install","npm install","npm ci","pnpm install","bun install","run-script")
| where ActingProcessName has_any (SuspectChildren)
   or (ActingProcessName has "node" and ActingProcessCommandLine has_any (" -e ","--eval","child_process","trufflehog"))
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername,
          ParentProcessName, ParentCmd=ParentProcessCommandLine,
          ActingProcessName, ChildCmd=ActingProcessCommandLine, Hashes
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: npm Lifecycle Hook Spawning Shell or Network LOLBin (Shai-Hulud Worm Behavior)
id: b4e9c3d2-8f5e-4b0d-ac20-6e9f3d5b2c7a
description: Catches the primary execution vector for Shai-Hulud 3.0 and prior Shai-Hulud variants — an npm/yarn/pnpm/bun lifecycle hook (postinstall/preinstall) invoking a shell, downloader, or git client during install. Equivalent to the behavior Snyk's `ignore-scripts=true` mitigation blocks.
references:
  - https://snyk.io/blog/shai-hulud-3-0/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21
tags:
  - attack.initial_access
  - attack.t1195.002
  - attack.execution
  - attack.t1059.007
  - attack.persistence
  - attack.t1546
logsource:
  category: process_creation
detection:
  selection_parent:
    ParentImage|endswith:
      - '\node.exe'
      - '\npm.cmd'
      - '\pnpm.cmd'
      - '\yarn.cmd'
      - '\bun.exe'
      - '/node'
      - '/npm'
      - '/pnpm'
      - '/yarn'
      - '/bun'
    ParentCommandLine|contains:
      - 'postinstall'
      - 'preinstall'
      - 'npm-cli.js'
      - 'pnpm-cli'
      - 'yarn install'
      - 'npm install'
      - 'npm ci'
      - 'pnpm install'
      - 'bun install'
      - 'run-script'
  selection_child:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\powershell.exe'
      - '\pwsh.exe'
      - '\cmd.exe'
      - '\curl.exe'
      - '\wget.exe'
      - '\bitsadmin.exe'
      - '\certutil.exe'
      - '\git.exe'
      - '\schtasks.exe'
      - '\reg.exe'
      - '/bash'
      - '/sh'
      - '/curl'
      - '/wget'
      - '/python'
      - '/python3'
  condition: selection_parent and selection_child
falsepositives:
  - Native modules using node-gyp / prebuild-install which legitimately spawn python and shell during install
  - husky installing git hooks during postinstall (parent invokes git.exe)
  - Cypress / Puppeteer / Playwright postinstall downloading browser binaries via curl/wget
  - Electron-builder postinstall fetching platform binaries
level: medium
source:(windows.sysmon OR windows.security OR linux.auditd) (@ParentImage:(*\node.exe OR *\npm.cmd OR *\pnpm.cmd OR *\yarn.cmd OR *\bun.exe OR */node OR */npm OR */pnpm OR */yarn OR */bun) OR @ParentBaseFileName:(node.exe OR npm.cmd OR pnpm.cmd OR yarn.cmd OR bun.exe)) (@ParentCommandLine:(*postinstall* OR *preinstall* OR *npm-cli.js* OR *"yarn install"* OR *"npm install"* OR *"npm ci"* OR *"pnpm install"* OR *"bun install"*)) (@Image:(*\powershell.exe OR *\pwsh.exe OR *\cmd.exe OR *\curl.exe OR *\wget.exe OR *\bitsadmin.exe OR *\certutil.exe OR *\git.exe OR *\schtasks.exe OR *\reg.exe OR */bash OR */sh OR */curl OR */wget) OR @process.executable.path:(*/bash OR */sh OR */curl OR */wget OR */python OR */python3))
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2
| ParentBaseFileName=/(node|npm|pnpm|yarn|bun)(\.exe)?$/i
| regex("(postinstall|preinstall|npm-cli\.js|pnpm-cli|yarn install|npm install|npm ci|pnpm install|bun install|run-script)", field=ParentCommandLine, flags="i")
| ImageFileName=/(powershell|pwsh|cmd|curl|wget|bitsadmin|certutil|git|schtasks|reg|bash|sh|python|python3)(\.exe)?$/i
| NOT UserName=/\$$/
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ParentBaseFileName, ImageFileName, CommandLine, ParentCommandLine], function=count())

[LLM] Shai-Hulud 3.0 'Goldox-T3chs' GitHub exfiltration marker observed Actions on Objectives High
Hunts for the verbatim attribution strings 'Goldox-T3chs' and 'Only Happy Girl' that the malware embeds in the description of attacker-owned GitHub repositories used as the exfil sink. Because the marker is hard-coded by the threat actor and ships with the malicious package, any sighting in cloud-app audit, script content, file writes, or process command lines is extremely high-fidelity. Rationale: Snyk article verbatim quote: "data was intended to be exfiltrated to GitHub repositories labeled with the description: 'Goldox-T3chs: Only Happy Girl'". This is a hand-picked attacker attribution string — virtually zero legitimate-use overlap. Detection covers cloud audit (where the marker first surfaces when the malware creates the dropper repo), endpoint script content (the marker is hardcoded in the dropper JS), and process command lines (any tool that interpolates the marker — gh CLI, curl to api.github.com). Cross-checked against: • https://snyk.io/blog/shai-hulud-3-0/
ATT&CKT1567 T1567.001 T1552.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem Web
let marker = dynamic(["Goldox-T3chs","Only Happy Girl","Goldox"]);
let cloudHits = CloudAppEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(60d)
    | where Application has_any ("GitHub","github") or ApplicationId == 11161
    | where tostring(RawEventData) has_any (marker) or ObjectName has_any (marker) or tostring(ActivityObjects) has_any (marker)
    | project Timestamp, Source="CloudAppEvents", Application, ActionType, Account=AccountDisplayName, IPAddress, Detail=tostring(RawEventData);
let procHits = DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(60d)
    | where ProcessCommandLine has_any (marker) or InitiatingProcessCommandLine has_any (marker)
    | project Timestamp, Source="DeviceProcessEvents", Application=DeviceName, ActionType=FileName, Account=AccountName, IPAddress="", Detail=ProcessCommandLine;
let fileHits = DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(60d)
    | where FileName has_any (marker) or FolderPath has_any (marker)
    | project Timestamp, Source="DeviceFileEvents", Application=DeviceName, ActionType, Account=InitiatingProcessAccountName, IPAddress="", Detail=strcat(FolderPath,"\\",FileName);
cloudHits | union procHits, fileHits | order by Timestamp desc
let marker = dynamic(["Goldox-T3chs","Only Happy Girl"]);
let fileHits = ImFileEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
    | where ActingProcessCommandLine has_any (marker) or TargetFilePath has_any (marker) or TargetFileName has_any (marker)
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="ImFileEvent", Host=DvcHostname, Account=ActorUsername, Detail=coalesce(ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetFilePath);
let procHits = ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
    | where ActingProcessCommandLine has_any (marker) or ParentProcessCommandLine has_any (marker)
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="ImProcessCreate", Host=DvcHostname, Account=ActorUsername, Detail=ActingProcessCommandLine;
let auditHits = AuditLogs
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
    | where tostring(TargetResources) has_any (marker) or tostring(AdditionalDetails) has_any (marker) or tostring(InitiatedBy) has_any (marker)
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="AuditLogs", Host="", Account=tostring(InitiatedBy), Detail=tostring(TargetResources);
let webHits = ImWebSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
    | where Url has_any (marker) or HttpRequestHeaderUserAgent has_any (marker)
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="ImWebSession", Host=DvcHostname, Account=SrcUsername, Detail=Url;
fileHits | union procHits, auditHits, webHits | order by TimeGenerated desc
title: Shai-Hulud 3.0 'Goldox-T3chs' Attribution Marker Observed
id: c5fad4e3-9a6f-4c1e-bd31-7faf4e6c3d8b
description: High-fidelity hunt for the verbatim threat-actor attribution strings 'Goldox-T3chs' and 'Only Happy Girl' that Shai-Hulud 3.0 embeds in the description of attacker-controlled GitHub exfil repositories. Any sighting in process command lines, file paths, or script content is extremely likely true positive.
references:
  - https://snyk.io/blog/shai-hulud-3-0/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21
tags:
  - attack.exfiltration
  - attack.t1567.001
  - attack.credential_access
  - attack.t1552.001
logsource:
  category: process_creation
detection:
  selection:
    CommandLine|contains:
      - 'Goldox-T3chs'
      - 'Only Happy Girl'
  condition: selection
falsepositives:
  - Threat-intel researchers / SOC analysts copy-pasting the marker into a console for hunting (whitelist analyst workstations)
level: critical
(@CommandLine:(*Goldox-T3chs* OR *"Only Happy Girl"*) OR @ParentCommandLine:(*Goldox-T3chs* OR *"Only Happy Girl"*) OR @process.command_line:(*Goldox-T3chs* OR *"Only Happy Girl"*) OR @TargetFilename:*Goldox* OR @http.url:*Goldox* OR @evt.description:*Goldox-T3chs*)
(#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 OR #event_simpleName=SyntheticProcessRollup2 OR #event_simpleName=ScriptControlScanInfo OR #event_simpleName=FileWritten OR #event_simpleName=AsepValueUpdate)
| regex("(Goldox-T3chs|Only Happy Girl|Goldox)", field=CommandLine, flags="i") OR regex("(Goldox-T3chs|Only Happy Girl)", field=ScriptContent, flags="i") OR regex("Goldox", field=TargetFileName, flags="i") OR regex("Goldox", field=RegStringValue, flags="i")
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, #event_simpleName], function=count())
CLOUD
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process="*Goldox-T3chs*" OR Processes.process="*Only Happy Girl*") by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | append [| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem where (Filesystem.file_path="*Goldox*" OR Filesystem.file_name="*goldox*") by Filesystem.dest Filesystem.user Filesystem.file_name Filesystem.file_path | `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`] | append [| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Web where (Web.url="*Goldox-T3chs*" OR Web.http_user_agent="*Goldox*") by Web.src Web.user Web.url Web.dest | `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`] | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process="*Goldox-T3chs*" OR Processes.process="*Only Happy Girl*") by Processes.dest Processes.user Processes.process_name Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | append [| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem where (Filesystem.file_path="*Goldox*" OR Filesystem.file_name="*goldox*") by Filesystem.dest Filesystem.user Filesystem.file_name Filesystem.file_path | `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`] | append [| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Web where (Web.url="*Goldox-T3chs*" OR Web.http_user_agent="*Goldox*") by Web.src Web.user Web.url Web.dest | `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`] | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command Exploitation High
Encoded or obfuscated PowerShell — common across loaders, recon, and post-exploitation.
ATT&CKT1059.001 T1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\pwsh.exe"
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(-enc|encodedcommand|frombase64string|-nop|-w\s+hidden|invoke-expression|iex\s*\(|downloadstring|net\.webclient)"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName,
          CommandLine, ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Obfuscated / Encoded PowerShell Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d08
status: experimental
description: |
    PowerShell launched with -EncodedCommand, base64 strings, hidden
    window, no-profile, bypass policy, IEX / Invoke-Expression / Net
    .WebClient.DownloadString — combinations indicating evasion.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1059/001/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1027/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1059.001
    - attack.t1027
    - attack.execution
    - attack.defense-evasion
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_image:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
    selection_args:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '-enc '
            - 'EncodedCommand'
            - 'FromBase64String'
            - 'Invoke-Expression'
            - 'IEX('
            - 'DownloadString'
            - 'Net.WebClient'
            - '-w hidden'
            - '-WindowStyle Hidden'
            - '-nop'
            - '-NoProfile'
            - '-ep bypass'
            - '-ExecutionPolicy Bypass'
    condition: selection_image and selection_args
falsepositives:
    - DevOps tooling that legitimately uses encoded command for
      cross-shell escaping
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*-enc *" OR Processes.process="*EncodedCommand*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64String*" OR Processes.process="*-nop*"
        OR Processes.process="*-w hidden*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*IEX(*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*Net.WebClient*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe")
      AND (Processes.process="*-enc *" OR Processes.process="*EncodedCommand*"
        OR Processes.process="*FromBase64String*" OR Processes.process="*-nop*"
        OR Processes.process="*-w hidden*" OR Processes.process="*Invoke-Expression*"
        OR Processes.process="*IEX(*" OR Processes.process="*DownloadString*"
        OR Processes.process="*Net.WebClient*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

CISA KEV: CVE-2025-14847 — MongoDB and MongoDB Server Improper Handling of Length Parameter Inconsistency Vulnerability

CISA KEV
2025-12-29 1 use case 0 techniques 2 kill-chain phases detected

MongoDB Server contains an improper handling of length parameter inconsistency vulnerability in Zlib compressed protocol headers. This vulnerability may allow a read of uninitialized heap memory by an unauthenticated client. Vendor: MongoDB, Product: MongoDB and MongoDB Server. Federal patch due: 2026-01-19.

Actions on Objectives
—
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-14847")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-14847")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc
APPLICATION
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Vulnerabilities
    where Vulnerabilities.signature IN ("CVE-2025-14847")
    by Vulnerabilities.dest, Vulnerabilities.signature, Vulnerabilities.severity, Vulnerabilities.cve
| `drop_dm_object_name(Vulnerabilities)`
| sort - severity
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Vulnerabilities
    where Vulnerabilities.signature IN ("CVE-2025-14847")
    by Vulnerabilities.dest, Vulnerabilities.signature, Vulnerabilities.severity, Vulnerabilities.cve
| `drop_dm_object_name(Vulnerabilities)`

Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] Vulnerable WindowsCodecs.dll (CVE-2025-50165) present on endpoint · [LLM] Process crash with faulting module WindowsCodecs.dll (CVE-2025-50165 exploit attempt) · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Revisiting CVE-2025-50165: A critical flaw in Windows Imaging Component
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
[LLM] Vulnerable WindowsCodecs.dll (CVE-2025-50165) present on endpoint Exploitation High
Surface hosts running WindowsCodecs.dll versions between 10.0.26100.0 and 10.0.26100.4945 that contain the uninitialized function pointer dereference reachable via 12-bit/16-bit JPG re-encoding. Pivots on the exact vulnerable SHA-1 from ESET's analysis and the Defender TVM CVE catalogue. Rationale: ESET names both the vulnerable build (10.0.26100.4768, SHA-1 5887D96565749067564BABCD3DC5D107AB6666BD) and the patched build (10.0.26100.4946, SHA-1 4EC1DC0431432BC318E78C520387911EC44F84FC). Hash-pinning gives near-zero false positives versus generic CVE banners. Cross-checked with the original Zscaler ThreatLabz disclosure and Microsoft MSRC's CVE entry. Cross-checked against: • https://www.zscaler.com/blogs/security-research/technical-analysis-cve-2025-50165 • https://msrc.microsoft.com/update-guide/vulnerability/CVE-2025-50165
ATT&CKT1203
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
// Path A: TVM catalogue hit for CVE-2025-50165
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where CveId == "CVE-2025-50165"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, SoftwareVendor, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| union (
    // Path B: image-load of the vulnerable WindowsCodecs.dll hash from ESET (10.0.26100.4768)
    DeviceImageLoadEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
    | where FileName =~ "WindowsCodecs.dll"
    | where SHA1 =~ "5887D96565749067564BABCD3DC5D107AB6666BD"
    | summarize FirstSeen=min(Timestamp), LastSeen=max(Timestamp), LoadingProcs=make_set(InitiatingProcessFileName, 25) by DeviceName, FolderPath, SHA1
)
| order by DeviceName asc

| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where CveId == "CVE-2025-50165"
| project TimeGenerated, DeviceName, SoftwareVendor, SoftwareName, SoftwareVersion, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by DeviceName asc
title: Vulnerable WindowsCodecs.dll Loaded (CVE-2025-50165)
id: c6f1a4e2-50b4-4f17-9b0d-50165cve2025
description: Detects load of WindowsCodecs.dll version 10.0.26100.4768 (SHA1 5887D96565749067564BABCD3DC5D107AB6666BD) — the vulnerable build identified by ESET for CVE-2025-50165 uninitialized function pointer in jpeg_finish_compress.

[LLM] Process crash with faulting module WindowsCodecs.dll (CVE-2025-50165 exploit attempt) Exploitation Medium
Hunts for application crashes where the faulting module is WindowsCodecs.dll inside a process that handles JPG re-encoding (Explorer thumbnails, image editors, Office, browsers). The ESET analysis shows the crash signature is dereference of 0xBAADF00D inside jpeg_finish_compress when a 12/16-bit JPG is re-encoded — a process crash with WindowsCodecs.dll on the stack is a high-fidelity exploit-attempt indicator on unpatched hosts. Rationale: ESET demonstrates the crash signature inside WindowsCodecs.dll's jpeg_finish_compress — 0xBAADF00D dereference. Application Error EID 1000 / WerFault invocations naming WindowsCodecs.dll as faulting module in JPG-handling parents (Explorer thumbnail extraction via dllhost.exe, Photos.exe, Office, browsers, Acrobat, Photoshop) are scoped to plausible attack vectors per Microsoft's WIC re-encode code path. The list of JPG-handling parents is from MS WIC documentation linked in the article. Cross-checked against: • https://www.zscaler.com/blogs/security-research/technical-analysis-cve-2025-50165 • https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/wic/-wic-codec-jpegmetadataencoding
ATT&CKT1203 T1059
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(14d)
| where FileName =~ "WerFault.exe"
| where ProcessCommandLine has "WindowsCodecs" or ProcessCommandLine has "windowscodecs.dll"
| extend FaultingHost = InitiatingProcessFileName
| where FaultingHost in~ ("explorer.exe","thumbnailcache.exe","dllhost.exe","photos.exe","mspaint.exe","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe","outlook.exe","chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","acrord32.exe","acrobat.exe","photoshop.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, FaultingHost, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, ProcessCommandLine, AccountName, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| join kind=leftouter (
    DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities | where CveId == "CVE-2025-50165" | project DeviceName, IsVulnerable=true
) on DeviceName
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(14d)
| where EventID == 1000   // Application Error
| where EventData has "WindowsCodecs.dll" or EventData has "windowscodecs.dll"
| extend FaultingApp = tostring(extract(@"Faulting application name: ([^,\s]+)", 1, EventData))
| extend FaultingModule = tostring(extract(@"Faulting module name: ([^,\s]+)", 1, EventData))
| extend ExceptionCode = tostring(extract(@"Exception code: (0x[0-9A-Fa-f]+)", 1, EventData))
| where FaultingApp in~ ("explorer.exe","dllhost.exe","Photos.exe","mspaint.exe","WINWORD.EXE","EXCEL.EXE","POWERPNT.EXE","OUTLOOK.EXE","chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","AcroRd32.exe","Acrobat.exe","Photoshop.exe")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, FaultingApp, FaultingModule, ExceptionCode, EventData
| order by TimeGenerated desc

#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 ImageFileName=/\\WerFault\.exe$/i CommandLine=/WindowsCodecs/i

   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.

Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).

Installation. [LLM] NosyDoor AppDomainManager hijack: UevAppMonitor.exe executing from non-standard path Installation High
Hunts the NosyDoor stage-2 LOLBin: the legitimate UevAppMonitor.exe is normally only present in C:\Windows\System32, but LongNosedGoblin's dropper copies it to C:\Windows\Microsoft.NET\Framework so it loads a sibling .config that triggers AppDomainManager injection from SharedReg.dll. Execution of UevAppMonitor.exe from any path other than System32 is highly indicative of NosyDoor stage-2. Rationale: UevAppMonitor.exe is a real Microsoft UE-V binary that ships only in System32; ESET explicitly notes the dropper copies it to C:\Windows\Microsoft.NET\Framework, then loads SharedReg.dll through a crafted .config (AppDomainManager injection). Cross-checked against The Hacker News and SecurityWeek coverage which restate the same path / DLL. Cross-checked against: • https://thehackernews.com/2025/12/china-aligned-threat-group-uses-windows.html • https://www.securityweek.com/chinese-apt-longnosedgoblin-targeting-asian-governments/ • https://www.helpnetsecurity.com/2025/12/18/eset-china-aligned-apt-group-policy/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1574/014/
ATT&CKT1574.014 T1218 T1036.005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
// LongNosedGoblin NosyDoor stage-2 — UevAppMonitor.exe is a legitimate Windows binary that *only* ships in System32. Execution from anywhere else (especially Microsoft.NET\Framework) means a copy was staged by the dropper to side-load SharedReg.dll via AppDomainManager.
let UevExec =
    DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FileName =~ "UevAppMonitor.exe"
    | where not(FolderPath startswith @"C:\Windows\System32\")
    | where not(FolderPath startswith @"C:\Windows\SysWOW64\")
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
              InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
              InitiatingProcessFolderPath, SHA256;
let SharedRegLoad =
    DeviceImageLoadEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where InitiatingProcessFileName =~ "UevAppMonitor.exe"
    | where FileName =~ "SharedReg.dll"
    | where not(FolderPath startswith @"C:\Windows\WinSxS\")
    | project Timestamp, DeviceName, FolderPath, FileName, SHA256,
              InitiatingProcessFolderPath;
UevExec
| union SharedRegLoad
| order by Timestamp desc
// LongNosedGoblin NosyDoor stage-2 — legitimate UevAppMonitor.exe staged outside System32
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where NewProcessName endswith @"\UevAppMonitor.exe"
| where not(NewProcessName startswith @"C:\Windows\System32\")
| where not(NewProcessName startswith @"C:\Windows\SysWOW64\")
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName, ProcessId, TokenElevationType
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] NosyDoor persistence: scheduled task 'OneDrive Reporting Task-S-1-5-21-' under Microsoft folder Installation High
NosyDoor's dropper persists by creating a scheduled task with the literal prefix 'OneDrive Reporting Task-S-1-5-21-' followed by a random GUID, registered under the Microsoft task folder, executing the relocated UevAppMonitor.exe at startup. The naming pattern is article-unique and not used by Microsoft OneDrive itself. Rationale: ESET reports the dropper creates a Windows scheduled task literally named 'OneDrive Reporting Task-S-1-5-21-<GUID>' under the Microsoft task folder to autorun the relocated UevAppMonitor.exe at startup. The literal prefix plus the SID-style suffix is article-unique — legitimate OneDrive tasks do not embed an S-1-5-21 SID in their name. Cross-checked against: • https://thehackernews.com/2025/12/china-aligned-threat-group-uses-windows.html • https://cyberpress.org/apt-hackers-china-malware/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1053/005/
ATT&CKT1053.005 T1036.005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
// LongNosedGoblin NosyDoor persistence — scheduled task with literal prefix "OneDrive Reporting Task-S-1-5-21-" registered under \Microsoft\ task folder.
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated", "FileRenamed")
| where FolderPath has @"\Windows\System32\Tasks\Microsoft\"
| where FileName startswith "OneDrive Reporting Task-S-1-5-21-"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, FolderPath, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName,
          InitiatingProcessAccountSid, SHA256
| order by Timestamp desc
// LongNosedGoblin NosyDoor — TaskScheduler 4698 with NosyDoor task-name prefix

| where EventData has "OneDrive Reporting Task-S-1-5-21-"          // article-specific prefix
| extend XmlData = parse_xml(EventData)
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, SubjectUserName, SubjectDomainName,
          SubjectLogonId, EventData
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] NosyDoor dropper file artefacts in C:\Windows\Microsoft.NET\Framework Installation High
Hunts the four-file drop the NosyDoor stage-1 dropper writes into C:\Windows\Microsoft.NET\Framework — SharedReg.dll, log.cached, netfxsbs9.hkf and UevAppMonitor.exe.config — names deliberately chosen to blend with legitimate SharedReg12.dll / netfxsbs12.hkf. None of these filenames are produced by genuine .NET Framework installation/servicing, so any user-mode write into that directory is a strong NosyDoor staging signal. Rationale: ESET names the exact four files the dropper writes to C:\Windows\Microsoft.NET\Framework (SharedReg.dll, log.cached, netfxsbs9.hkf, UevAppMonitor.exe.config) and explicitly notes their goal is to blend with the genuine SharedReg12.dll / netfxsbs12.hkf — so the '9' vs '12' suffix and the unsuffixed SharedReg.dll are themselves diagnostic. The 'co-occurrence ≥ 2 of N' threshold (skipping Sigma because that's a count predicate) keeps FP rate low while catching variants where one filename rotates. Cross-checked against: • https://thehackernews.com/2025/12/china-aligned-threat-group-uses-windows.html • https://www.securityweek.com/chinese-apt-longnosedgoblin-targeting-asian-governments/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1574/014/
ATT&CKT1036.005 T1574.014
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
// LongNosedGoblin NosyDoor dropper — writes a known set of artefacts into C:\Windows\Microsoft.NET\Framework so the relocated UevAppMonitor.exe loads SharedReg.dll via .config-driven AppDomainManager injection.
let NosyDoorFiles = dynamic([
    "SharedReg.dll",
    "log.cached",
    "netfxsbs9.hkf",            // typo of legitimate netfxsbs12.hkf
    "UevAppMonitor.exe.config",
    "UevAppMonitor.exe",         // staged copy from System32
    "error.txt"                  // dropped on stage-2 decryption errors
]);
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated", "FileModified", "FileRenamed")
| where FolderPath in~ (@"C:\Windows\Microsoft.NET\Framework", @"C:\Windows\Microsoft.NET\Framework64")
   or FolderPath endswith @"\Microsoft.NET\Framework"
   or FolderPath endswith @"\Microsoft.NET\Framework64"
| where FileName in~ (NosyDoorFiles)
// Exclude legitimate .NET servicing — the genuine SharedReg DLL is named SharedReg12.dll, not SharedReg.dll
| where not(InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("TrustedInstaller.exe", "msiexec.exe", "setup.exe") and InitiatingProcessFolderPath startswith @"C:\Windows\")
| summarize FileSet = make_set(FileName), FirstSeen = min(Timestamp), LastSeen = max(Timestamp),
            Writers = make_set(InitiatingProcessFileName)
  by DeviceName, FolderPath, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| where array_length(FileSet) >= 2     // at least two of the four sibling artefacts
| order by LastSeen desc
// Sentinel equivalent via ASIM ImFileEvent — NosyDoor dropper artefact set
let NosyDoorFiles = dynamic(["SharedReg.dll","log.cached","netfxsbs9.hkf","UevAppMonitor.exe.config","UevAppMonitor.exe","error.txt"]);
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
| where TargetFilePath has @"\Windows\Microsoft.NET\Framework"
| where TargetFileName in~ (NosyDoorFiles)
| where not(ActingProcessName has_any ("TrustedInstaller.exe","msiexec.exe"))
| summarize FileSet = make_set(TargetFileName), FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen = max(TimeGenerated),
            Writers = make_set(ActingProcessName)
          by DvcHostname, ActorUsername
| where array_length(FileSet) >= 2
| order by LastSeen desc
